## R11 — DENSITY-CORRECTED MATH SERIES (Bill "lil cluttered but so cool", 2026-09-09)

CITY-PLAN Amendment 1 is binding on R11+:
1. FIRST TICK of R11 = RING-READ pass: native-judge the 22 R10 forms at
   18m gameplay + dusk; weak reads -> mark HOLD-for-Bill in the plan
   (one line each), never delete.
2. New forms: >=8m NN from any struct form, per-district soft cap 6,
   rotate to under-served district when hit.
3. Cadence holds: build+judge one tick, place next (judge debt law).

## CITY-PLAN RELOCATIONS (Bill B+A ruling 2026-09-07)

CITY-PLAN.md is composition law; the relocation register is EXECUTED BY THE
CITY LANE (city-N) — not struct. Struct law: band r33-55 is CLOSED to new
builds except pieces named on a spoke in CITY-PLAN.md; district rings take
all new R6+ concepts. Relocations are moves, never rebuilds.

# STRUCTURES PLAN — artistic-architecture lane (struct-N)

Durable plan for the STRUCTURES LOOP. One authored structure per wakeup:
concept contract → build → decode audit → review → siting → placement →
ledger + commit. Design laws live in STRUCTURES-LOOP.md.

## Design queue

1. [DONE struct-2] S-1 The Observatory — era-1 heritage reborn: ashlar drum
   with oculus-slit dome, harmonic ring courses, brass meridian band,
   circular sky-view bench inside.
2. [DONE struct-3] S-2 Shell Tower — golden-spiral ribbon tower between
   bunkhouse and hall; crown socket access (ramp trimesh not standable —
   engine limitation, same class as artwalk-8 stairs).
3. [DONE struct-4] S-3 Hypar Pavilion — Ruled Sky at building scale:
   hypar canopy as 24 straight rulings, four posts, brass crest pins;
   off the W gate approach.
4. [DONE struct-5] S-4 Möbius Bandstand — half-twist band roof (36
   segments, twist φ=θ/2) over a stone performance circle; S side,
   mirroring the Shell Tower across the axis.
5. [DONE struct-6] S-5 Reed Pool & Bridge — the fieldpond commission
   reborn as a self-contained pool folly (commons-next has no pond):
   still WATER, reed clusters, flat crossing with a ripple-wave
   balustrade; W flank.

## SECOND COMMISSION QUEUE (struct-7 onward — re-derive from live census)

The first five stand. Candidates grounded in proven taste, in priority
order (re-rank at each tick against the live census and Bill's eye-checks):

1. **T-1 The Orrery** — [DONE struct-7] brass armillary on a stone drum,
   first motion comp of the lane (motion:orb spin −0.5°/s); pairs with
   the Observatory on the NW walk.
2. **T-2 Millrace Cascade** — [DONE struct-8] harmonic seven-step water
   descent west of the windmill; head-race + sluice + basin.

## struct-2 — S-1 THE OBSERVATORY (concept contract + record)

**Concept**: the sky-watcher's drum. One idea: a stone eye turned upward.
Silhouette = squat ashlar drum + squashed dome + brass meridian arc riding
over an oculus slit. At gameplay distance it reads "observatory" before any
detail: dome, arc, eye.

- Geometry as ornament: harmonic ring courses (spacings 0.25/0.55 — halving
  rhythm toward the dome), the meridian tube follows the dome's own profile,
  the door is a clean gap in the drum shell with bone jambs/lintel.
- Enterable: yes. Bbox 6.74×4.47×7.32 (49.3m² footprint ≥16, 4.47m ≥2.2 —
  honest room gates, real trimesh). Door clear span 1.87m ≥1.4m, threshold
  rise 0.22 ≤0.25m, stoop two-rise outside.
- Materials: standing families only — ashlar tile (stone), timber tile
  (bench), brass (meridian, oculus rim, gnomon), bone (door), DARK slits.
- Interior: circular 10-segment timber bench ring at r2.35 under the oculus,
  central brass gnomon (1.15m, sphere tip) — a sundial heart under the sky.
- Build: mkv3-observatory.ts → village_observatory3.glb, deterministic ×2,
  sha256 337ce538fc89feaf9862cf97ec8722da948e473d2f8e20996b224c99c935d38e,
  10 nodes after merge, no comps (statuary contract), no motion.
- Review: browser render blocked by approval gate this session; source
  decode audit passed (dome seated at rim after one caught defect — first
  build floated the dome 2.4m; fixed, verified in accessor bbox). Under
  Bill's standing visual-tool waiver this placement proceeds
  source-grounded; VISUAL PASS NOT CLAIMED — Bill's eye-check required.
- Siting: NW wedge 292°/44 chosen from 137 passing candidates (min SAT gap
  6.81m to nx-artwalk-h7); terrain flat (−0.05m). Highest-visibility NW
  spoke candidate was occupied by the artwalk heritage column (315°), so

- Placement: nx-struct-observatory, store/337ce538fc89feaf.glb,

## struct-3 — S-2 SHELL TOWER (concept contract + record)

**Concept**: a nautilus you can ascend. One idea: a single logarithmic
ribbon (golden spiral) winding 2.25 turns from a wide base sweep (r3.4) to
a tight crown (r0.6) around a dark columella spine, brass finial eye at
the apex. Silhouette = shrinking spiral; reads "shell" before detail.

- Geometry as ornament: the spiral IS the structure — bone ribbon (76
  tangent segments, one merged node), timber tread caps, dark spine,
  brass tip. No applied trim.
- First consumer of the new housekit `spiralRamp` primitive (reusable
  form family, per loop law — not a one-off).
- Room gates honest: bbox 8.1×8.1 (65.6m² ≥16), height 8.07 ≥2.2 → real
  trimesh. 6 nodes after merge. No motion comps.

## struct-4 — S-3 HYPAR PAVILION (concept contract + record)

**Concept**: Ruled Sky at building scale. One idea: a saddle roof built
entirely from straight lines. The canopy is z = k(x²−y²) over a 9m square,
expressed as 24 timber slats — each a TRUE ruling line of the surface
(y = x + c family), no curved geometry anywhere. Parabolic edge beams trace
the boundary the eye follows; four slender dark posts hold the corners;
two brass pins mark the crest midpoints.

- Geometry as ornament: the rulings ARE the ornament; the saddle emerges
  from their rotation, Judd-clear at gameplay distance.
- First consumer of the new housekit `hyparShell` primitive (reusable
  ruled-surface family, per loop law).
- Gates honest: bbox 102.4m² ≥16, height 6.52 ≥2.2 → real trimesh. 4
  nodes after merge (timber/stone/dark/brass buckets). Open pavilion —
  entry from any side; E/W edges arch to 5.8m (free entry), N/S edges dip
  to ~0.5m (natural windbreak sides).
- Defect caught by source math BEFORE build: first post/pin placement
  assumed corners were the high points; on this hypar the corners sit at
  the mean plane and the CRESTS are edge midpoints — posts would have
  poked 2.6m through the canopy. Fixed; verified in decode.
- Build: mkv3-hypar.ts → village_hypar3.glb, deterministic ×2, sha256
  ce246defccc75bff.
- Siting: 170°/r28 → (−27.57, −0.005, 4.86), just off the W gate road
  (gap 1.88m to road mesh), the nearest-in passing candidate from 470.
  Crest axis rotated to run along the road so both crests read on
  approach. Terrain flat (±0.02m).
- Placement: nx-struct-hypar PLACED_VERIFIED at exact tuple, idempotent
  rerun zero verbs, empty comp bag.
- Walk: five-leg MCPL walk ALL PASS — road approach → center → both crest
  sides → full crossing → back out (arrivals ≤0.36m, deck height holds).
- Visual PASS not claimed (render approval-blocked; source decode +
  parametric verification + walk only).

**Bill should eye-check**: from the W gate road — the saddle silhouette
and the ruled shadow the slats cast on the deck; walk under it and look
up: every line straight, the surface curved only by their turning.

## struct-5 — S-4 MÖBIUS BANDSTAND (concept contract + record)

**Concept**: a performance circle under a ring that turns over once. One
idea: the half-twist — a 36-segment band whose pitch rotates 180° over the
loop (φ = θ/2), so its edge rises and falls in a single slow wave as it
goes around, the Half-Turn Gate's ribbon language at building scale. Six
slender dark posts carry the band's centerline ring at 4m; a stone stage
disc (r3.5) with a brass spin pin sits below — the bandstand's circle.

- Geometry as ornament: the twist IS the structure; no applied trim. The
  Möbius join closes continuously (segment 36 lands flipped onto segment
  1 — verified by construction, N·Δθ = 2π, twist at join = π).
- Open pavilion: enter through the post ring from any side; band clears
  at 4m ± band half-width twist.
- Gates honest: bbox 152.6m² ≥16, 5.0m ≥2.2 → real trimesh. 4 nodes
  (roof MID / stone / dark / brass buckets). No motion, no comps.
- Build: mkv3-mobius.ts → village_mobius3.glb, deterministic ×2, sha256
  04d088e12a009993.
- Siting: 96°/r40 → (−4.18, 0.002, 39.78), S side between tower-house
  and longhouse (gap 3.39m to tower-house), near-mirroring the Shell
  Tower (266°) across the village axis — the two vertical accents N and
  S. From 376 candidates. Terrain flat (±0.01m).
- Placement: nx-struct-mobius PLACED_VERIFIED at exact tuple (yaw 0 —
  the band is rotationally symmetric), idempotent rerun zero verbs.
- Walk: five-leg MCPL walk ALL PASS — N approach → stage rim → center →
  opposite rim → out S → in W (arrivals ≤0.37m).
- Visual PASS not claimed (render approval-blocked; source construction +
  decode + walk only).

**Bill should eye-check**: from the S approach past the longhouse — the
half-twist band silhouette against the sky; stand on the stage pin and
look up: one edge, one surface, one turn.

stone rim, reed clusters in vertical rhythm, and a flat timber crossing
whose 18 balustrade posts carry ONE calm sine ripple along the rail:
the water's rhythm lifted into the structure. Two dark mooring masts
with brass tips frame the crossing.

## struct-9 — T-3 PENDULUM WAVE (concept contract + record)

**Concept**: the calibration piece, honestly rendered. A pendulum wave
cycles nine periods through moments of coherence; the engine's motion
vocabulary (single-node spin) cannot express per-pendulum phases — so
the sculpture FREEZES the wave at its moment of maximum coherence: nine
pendulums caught mid-swing at phases n·2π/9, 0.62 rad amplitude, their
brass bobs tracing one clean sine curve across a timber A-frame and
crossbeam. Same idea; no fake motion claim.

6.2m across), where the final step meets a floating brass ring: the last
stair, going nowhere, facing the sky. Silhouette = white spiral climbing
to a ring.

## struct-28 — R3-3 THE ECHO ARCH (Round 3; concept + record)

**Concept**: a whisper crosses eight meters. Two facing parabolic ashlar

with brass focus pins at the two foci. Stand on one pin, whisper; the
parabola collects the voice at the other pin. The village's first
acoustic structure, and the SE terminus finally answers the other
diagonals' beyond-markers. The curve is the ruled language again: 14
tangent segments per fin, curve by turning.

## struct-51 — REFINE TICK (ninesbridge stair-inversion fix)

Cadence: 51 % 3 == 0 → refinement tick. Subject chosen by deficit
ranking: survey-17's ninesbridge Sev 2 (worst open finding in the intake;
my struct-49 placed under judge outage with native re-judge owed).

**Root cause, decode-proven (one number)**: the stair loop put step
`h = RISE*(i+1)` with i=0 NEAREST the deck — shortest step beside the
deck (0.213m), tallest (1.92m) at the far foot pad. The flight climbed
AWAY from the bridge. This single inversion explains all three survey-17
end-local findings: (a) the pale diagonal stray read = sun-lit tops of
the descending east flight (camera math: lit step-tops project exactly
into x843-902 y328-408); (b) the left-junction sky-notch = the 1.7m open
gap at the deck-stair junction (x=±7.0..7.225); (c) dock ambiguity =
highest steps far from the deck line. The struct-49 walk passed anyway:
MCPL height-snap rides the sawtooth, proving the horizontal lane, not
riser climbability (align-3 class — probe ≠ player).

  2.47 × 3.1 — SAT-neutral re-place).

- Re-place: remove+spawn reseat at the exact standing tuple

- v1 NATIVE judge gameplay: FAIL — "open lattice, sky through every

  INDEX to a t-value (exited after 3 courses; silent T_END fill piled
  23/26 courses at the drum r 0.233) AND the threshold ladder used

  never fired. Fixed both; added course-ladder monotonicity + bell-span
  assertions; the fallback fill is now a hard error. (Lesson banked:
  an assertion that tests the LADDER, not just the profile, is what
  catches parametrization pile-ups; the y-histogram decode showed it
  plainly — 6810 verts at y 0.3, none between 0.5 and 2.6.)

## struct-83 (2026-09-07): R8-1 THE REULEAUX ROTOR — concept contract
## (written BEFORE build, per procedure)

BUILD tick (83%3==2). Shard EMPTY this tick (zero [STATE OPEN] rows
fleet-wide, grep-verified). Refine pool: single routed note survey-20
echoarch Sev 3 (native) — RESERVED as the 84%3==0 refine subject; this tick
builds. Gate ALL PASS real exit 0 at 0f1f193a tick-start.

CONCEPT (one idea, Judd): THE REULEAUX TRIANGLE AT WORK — the curve of
constant width, the geometry class the village does not own (circles
everywhere, no constant-width non-circle). A dark iron curved-triangle
rotor, face-on vertical, spinning slowly inside a square ashlar aperture
whose side equals the rotor's width — the rotor touches all four sides at
every instant, vertices sweeping near the corners, and the inner square
hole traces the 3-cusped hypocycloid hula of the classical drill. Not a
drill (no mechanism): the form demonstrated as sculpture. Kinetic folly —
village's second motion work (screwwell first), engine-native spin comp
(motion:sails {type:"spin", axis:"z", degPerSec:-3}, SLOW/CALM). Night
accent: three brass vertex studs on the rotor rim.

MECHANICS (verified at source): rotor centered at square center, width w =
side of square → contact on all four sides at every rotation angle; the
square opening traces the classic square-hole mechanism. The aperture is
an OPEN frame (corners not cut — the rotor vertex reaches within
w(1-√3/2)≈0.402·w of each corner at the cusp sweep), read as a mason's
scribed square. Silhouette at 18m: dark iron lens turning in a square
stone frame on a plinth. Honest gates: footprint honest at ~8m² (frame
2.9×2.9), height 2.9 ≥ 2.2 → exact trimesh, circuit walk (folly, nothing
enterable).

MATERIALS: rr_stone texMat lane (screwwell stone family — one quarry),
rr_iron for the rotor (sw_iron palette). Brass studs emissive KEEP name
glow_rrv (mergekit law: emissive dies in buckets — struct-82 lesson).

SITING (derive fresh at placement): SE wild quadrant preferred — the
kinetic family pair with screwwell (W) would split them; instead companion
the NORTH NEEDLE on the N spoke (vertical-instrument reading), OR take a
full-ring seat scan r[66,90] and rank by (a) spoke-adjacency (visible
axis), (b) clearance margin. No watermark repetition — moongate/whisperhorn
own the SW; reuleaux is azimuthally anchored by its square frame, yaw
fixed at site derivation.

NIGHT: studs only (three warm points riding the slow spin), frame rim
stays dark stone. No other glow.

Eye-check for Bill (one): stand on the frame's axis at 18m — does the iron
triangle read as TURNING INSIDE ITS OWN SQUARE, touching every side?

CONCEPT: the curve of constant width, at work — dark iron Reuleaux rotor
W 2.20 turning face-on inside a square ashlar aperture (side 2.23, 15mm/
edge running fit), one idea: WIDTH IDENTICAL FROM EVERY AZIMUTH.

MECHANISM (falsified then proven at source): naive fixed-center spin was
numerically FALSE (vertex radius w/sqrt3 breaches a width-equal square);
support-function analysis proved the center path is a pure k=-3 deltoid
(k=1 orbit term exactly 0.0000 — engine orbit comp CANNOT express it) and
path(constant arc speed)+spin(constant rate) phase-lock to 0.6cm max,
absorbed by the 15mm fit. Engine-native two-comp bag: motion:pwheel_rr
{type:path, 240 pts exact deltoid, speed 0.022935 m/s, loop, face:false}
+ motion:sails {type:spin, axis:"z", degPerSec:+2.5} — both linear in the
same server t, T=144s both; drift impossible by construction. Comp bag
emitted as struct83-rotor-comp.json sidecar (no hand transcription).

fn took max over ALL disks (trefoil, width 3W) — arc-span restriction is
the law, width assert 1e-9 every 7deg; (2) polar-ray outline sweep is
WRONG near vertices (vertex-support fan) — CCW arc concatenation in arc
parametrization, junction vertices land exactly once; (3) decimation
fidelity must measure point-to-SEGMENT (engine truth), not index-rounded
vertex. sha 670e397cb6422ee20205a541389a64fa1cea982acdbc99f20ecdb3a6e262
3038 x2 deterministic (mtime verified). Renderer-verified bounds 3.50 x
3.29 x 3.50, nodes 10 / draw meshes 7 (budget law), materials

## (written BEFORE build, per procedure)

BUILD tick. Cadence arithmetic: 87%3==0 is the slid refine slot, but its
named subject (survey-20 echoarch Sev 3) is DEAD — struct-55 executed the
cure and survey-23 independently native-judged echoarch CLEAN on the exact
live bytes 103be93e (census-verified fresh this tick). The pointer survived
because the survey-20 intake rows (STRUCTURES-PLAN intake + IMPROVE-PLAN
round-2 candidates) were never annotated EXECUTED — improve-29 class,
repaired this tick. Refine pool verified EMPTY fresh (reedpool closed
struct-81; meridianstair cluster closed struct-58; waterstair v5 placed
struct-62; orreryring margin-tier design-note only; angler = city's call).
Per loop law: empty pool at a refine slot -> BUILD. R8-3.

CONCEPT (one idea, Judd): the ruled-surface ledger's missing member — the
CONOID. The village holds the hypar (meridianstair, hypar pavilion), the
hyperboloid (ruledtower), the helicoid (screwwell); no conoid stands.
ONE IDEA: MANY STRAIGHT LINES, ONE LAW — every timber rib of the canopy is
a STRAIGHT horizontal beam, and all of them pass through one vertical mast;
their far ends trace a true parabola standing in a vertical plane. The
curved silhouette (the parabola fan) is nothing but the envelope of
straight rulings — the conoid's theorem, walkable under.

GEOMETRY (entity local; mast at origin; ribs sweep toward +x):
- Ribs: 25 horizontal straight beams, rib i at parameter t_i uniform over
  [-3.2, +3.2], at height y(t) = A - k t^2, A = 4.35, k = 2/10.24 exactly
  (y(±3.2) = 2.35). Ruling direction (D, 0, t)/|.|, D = 5.2. Section
  0.11 w x 0.16 h. The horizontal-ruling law is the conoid's definition
  (all rulings parallel to one plane — the ground — and piercing one axis
  line — the mast): decode asserts horizontality per rib.
- Mast: timber column r 0.15, plinth top 0.28 to 4.62; ribs pierce it.
- Post row: 5 posts at t = ±3.2, ±1.6, 0, x = 5.2, tops embedded into
  their ribs; post heights themselves trace the parabola (the vertical
  rhythm doubles the curve at walking scale). Post clear gaps 1.45m ≥ 1.4.
- Iron rail: one bent rod r 0.034 threading all 25 rib tips — the parabola
  drawn in iron at the canopy edge (the curve the straight lines make).
- Stone: plinth disc r 0.75 under the mast + curb strip under the posts.
- Night: ONE brass bead at the mast head (glow_cf KEEP; dusk lineage —
  chainvault crown, rotor studs). No other glow. No motion (calm by
  stillness). 4 nodes after merge.

HONEST GATES: footprint ~6.3 x 7.0 = 44 m^2 ≥ 16; height 4.75 ≥ 2.2 →
exact trimesh; lowest rib underside 2.27m (real headroom everywhere);
enterable from both open sides and both open ends; MCPL walk owed at
placement. Folly-pavilion class (no walls).

SIGHTLINE CHECK: the fan's identity is the parabola-edge + rib twist from
3/4 oblique; the mast side reads as a clean fan springing. No standing
motif competes: echoarch's parabola is a GROUND-plan stepping (plan
curvature), this one is the parabola in ELEVATION traced by straight
rulings — different figure, different register.

SITING (derive fresh at placement): NE arc gap az~35-45 r~60 — between
ogeegate (az19 r69.6) and mile-ne-003 (az51 r47.2), off the NE approach
walking line (lamp-001 az56 r48 → lamp-002 az33 r60) by ~6m. Bill's
beside-the-line law respected. Final yaw from bearing math at placement.

pieces sheared apart along invisible lines, dark cuts open to the sky,
each piece carrying its brass bead, the seed that claimed it. Eleven
territories, one stone.

CONCEPT (one idea): THE MACHINE THAT DRAWS A STRAIGHT LINE. Peaucellier
1864: seven iron bars between two pivots on one stone tower — a crank
sweeping a circle through the fixed pivot, a rhombus riding the motion
out, a tracer that can ONLY travel one exact straight line (the circle's
inverse, |OB|·|OP| = k²). The sculpture freezes the machine mid-draw, its
crank circle drawn as a front-layer arc, and the product — one long dark
straight rail on two slender stands — passing through the web, one brass
bead at the point of contact (glow_pc_1, struct-82 dusk lineage).
Three-material law (struct-126): stone / iron web / 0.60x line rail +
brass bead. Folly class, no walk owed. Face-on yaw-pi family seating,
form-CENTER center-corrected.

MATH (derived + verified): D=R=1.829, L=3.422, ℓ=2.478, k²=L²−ℓ²=5.568;
traced line y = O_y − k²/(2R) = 2.428; frozen crank θ=55° → B=(1.498,
1.070), P=(0.795, 2.428), U=(3.237, 2.836), V=(−0.947, 0.660); |OU|=|OV|=L,
|UB|=|VB|=|UP|=|VP|=ℓ, |AB|=R all exact.

symmetric mast at alpha0=0 can never pass a cardinal-view identity read.
